TRANSITION ASYMPTOTICS FOR THE REAL SOLUTIONS OF THE
SINH-GORDON PAINLEV ´E III EQUATION
KENTA MIYAHARA AND MAXIM L. YATTSELEV
Abstract. We consider solutions of the sinh-Gordon Painlev´e III equation
𝑢𝑥𝑥` 1
𝑥𝑢𝑥“ sinh 𝑢
that are real on p0, 8q. They are parametrized by the monodromy parameter 𝑝P C,
|𝑝| ą 1, and an additional real parameter 𝑠R when 𝑝“ 8. Our previous joint work with
A. Its described the asymptotic behavior of these solutions as 𝑥Ñ 8. Here, we describe
the transition as 𝑥, 𝑝Ñ 8, 2 Imp𝑝q “ ´𝑠R, between singular solutions (|𝑝| ă 8) and
smooth solutions (𝑝“ 8). In short, if we parametrize |𝑝|2 “ 1 ` 𝑒2𝜘𝑥, then the smooth
exponential asymptotics of the solutions extends to the region 𝜘ą 1, with a change of the
leading order term at 𝜘“ 2; at 𝜘“ 1 the exponential behavior transitions into an elliptic
asymptotics, which holds for all 0 ă 𝜘ă 1; as 𝜘decays to zero, elliptic asymptotics
degenerates into trigonometric one, which holds for all 𝑝fixed.
Keywords: Painlev´e III equation; sinh-Gordon equation; isomonodromic deformation;
Riemann-Hilbert problem; steepest-descent method

Introduction and Main Results
This paper is a continuation of our previous work [IMY25], joint with Alexander Its,
where we studied the asymptotic behavior of real solutions of the sinh-Gordon Painlev´e III
equation
𝑢𝑥𝑥` 1
𝑥𝑢𝑥“ sinh 𝑢.
(1.0.1)
Our interest stems from the fact that (1.0.1) is the simplest tt*-Toda equation of Cecotti and
Vafa [CV91, CV92, CV93] whose analysis has been of much recent interest [GL12, GL14,
GIL15a, GIL15b, GIL20, GIL26, McI25].
We understand reality in the following sense. It is known that solutions of (1.0.1) can
have movable logarithmic singularities and that going around any logarithmic singularity
(via the complex plane) of a solution of (1.0.1) results in the addition of 2𝜋i to the solution.
So, when we speak of real solutions, we ignore all the acquired additions of 2𝜋i (since

TRANSITION ASYMPTOTICS FOR THE SINH-GORDON PIII
5
As was the case in all of the above-mentioned works, the precise statements of our
result are rather technical, but can be summarized as follows. We show that the asymptotic
formula (1.0.4) extends to the region 𝜘ą 2 of the p𝑥, 𝜘𝑥q-plane (the first part of the
exponential region on Figure 1). At 𝜘“ 2, the exponential nature of the decay remains the
same, but the leading order term changes from 𝐾0p𝑥q to 𝐼0p𝑥q, the modified Bessel function
of the first kind. This behavior persists for all 1 ă 𝜘ă 2 (the second part of the exponential
region on Figure 1). These results are described in Theorem 1.1. At 𝜘“ 1, exponential
decay changes into the behavior similar to (1.0.2), but with the sine function replaced by
a certain Jacobi elliptic function. This transition happens via “logarithmic cascades”, see
Theorem 1.2-1.3 and the “logarithmic cascade” region of Figure 1. Elliptic asymptotics
holds for each 0 ă 𝜘ă 1, see Theorem 1.4, which corresponds to the “elliptic” region on
Figure 1. Finally, when 𝜘decays to zero, elliptic asymptotics degenerates into (1.0.2), see
Theorem 1.5. This is the “trigonometric” region of Figure 1.
Figure 1 can also be understood as follows. Solution 𝑢p𝑥, 𝑝q for a fixed parameter 𝑝
corresponds to a horizontal line on Figure 1. Hence, for a very large fixed 𝑝, we see
that such a solution first resembles an exponentially decaying one with no singularities.
However, as 𝑥increases, singularities start to appear, but seemingly not regularly enough
to be described by an elliptic or trigonometric function (this corresponds to the horizontal
line passing through the logarithmic cascades region). Next, for even larger 𝑥, the behavior
of the solution is described with the help of elliptic functions, and eventually it becomes
trigonometric as in (1.0.2).
Throughout the paper, we always assume that (1.0.5) takes place. For a non-negative
quantity 𝑞, we let 𝑞` :“ maxt1, 𝑞u “ exptln` 𝑞u. We further set 𝜀:“ sgnpRep𝑝qq.
Our first theorem describes asymptotics of the real solutions of the sinh-Gordon Painlev´e
III equation (1.0.1) in the exponential region 𝜘ą 1.

This type of Riemann-Hilbert problem is well-known, see for example [DKM`99a,
DKM`99b]. We present its solution in full as the details of the construction are important
to the final result and are always problem-specific. Moreover, in the process we obtain very
technical asymptotic estimates of 𝑁p𝜆q needed for our proof.

5.4
Local Parametrices
Although we have observed that all the jump matrices in RHP 13 converge to the ones
in RHP 14 as 𝑥Ñ 8, the convergence is not uniform around ˘𝑎, ˘𝑎. To resolve this
issue, one needs to construct local parametrices in some neighborhoods of these points
that approximately solve RHP 13 locally within the neighborhoods and match the global
parametrix on the boundary.

follow [BDIK15]. Let

Proof of Theorem 1.5
The proof of Theorem 1.5 closely follows the proof of Theorem IMY given in [IMY25].
However, as compared to [IMY25], we use a slightly different jump factorization, which
leads to a simpler construction of the global parametrix. On the other hand, since the
monodromy parameter 𝐵R is now a function of 𝑥, we need a more precise analysis of the
local parametrices. In that, we follow the line of arguments developed in [Bot17].
Similarly to all the previous sections, we start with a gauge transformation of RHP 1.
